# Skills · F · part 79

776 entries, fse-ch02-reading-code to fuhao-learning-loop. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **fse-ch02-reading-code**: Playbook for understanding unfamiliar or inherited codebases: reframing legacy code, spotting reader biases, onboarding order (people, docs, ADRs, standards), software archeology (callers, landmarks, debugger, churn, generated diagrams), IDE navigation and inspections, reading tests as specification, and verifying AI explanations. Based on Chapter 2 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Use when a developer joins a project, must change brownfield code, asks "what does this code do?", wants a code-reading study plan, or relies on AI to explain a repository. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch02-reading-code))
- **fse-ch03-writing-code**: Guidance for writing code humans can read, based on Chapter 3 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Covers reuse before building, quality metrics and analyzers, cohesion and coupling, composition, short methods, naming, comments versus tests and contracts, avoiding clever code, humane code reviews, and learning languages. Use when deciding whether to write or reuse code, refactoring or reviewing code (human or AI-generated), adding linters to a legacy project, choosing metrics, or planning a developer's coding practice. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch03-writing-code))
- **fse-ch04-modeling**: Guidance for software modeling and diagramming as a communication tool, based on Chapter 4 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Covers why and when to diagram, choosing among context, component, class, sequence, deployment, security, data and workshop-style models, formality versus audience, keeping diagrams simple and accurate, color and templates, and tool choice including diagrams as code. Use when a developer asks which diagram to draw, explains a system to a given audience, reviews a diagram, picks a modeling tool, or documents an unfamiliar codebase. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch04-modeling))
- **fse-ch05-automated-testing**: Operational guidance on automated testing based on Chapter 5 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers why tests pay off (documentation, design feedback, confidence, regression safety), the test pyramid and choosing a mix, what not to test, code coverage as feedback, and the mechanics of unit, mocked, integration and end-to-end tests. Use when writing or reviewing tests, deciding which test level fits, fixing a bug, setting coverage policy, reading unfamiliar code through its tests, or coaching a developer to build a testing habit. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch05-automated-testing))
- **fse-ch06-exploring-unfamiliar-systems**: Guides onboarding to and safely changing existing or legacy codebases, based on Chapter 6 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Covers big-picture discovery, reading architecture from folder layout, entry points, request tracing, dependencies, homegrown frameworks, incremental mental models, characterization tests, the scout rule, and small reversible commits and PRs. Use when joining a new project, exploring an unfamiliar repo, tracing a bug through layers, planning a refactor in legacy code, or deciding whether cleanup is worth doing now. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch06-exploring-unfamiliar-systems))
- **fse-ch07-user-interface-design**: UI design guidance for software engineers: usability quality components and how to prioritize them by usage context, accessibility, i18n/l10n and culture, user research and safe defaults, contrast/repetition/alignment/proximity, discoverability, error messages and friction for destructive actions. Based on Chapter 7 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Use when building or reviewing a screen, form, dialog or settings page, writing error messages, designing delete flows, choosing defaults, checking color contrast, or planning accessibility and localization. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch07-user-interface-design))
- **fse-ch08-working-with-data**: Data-handling judgment for developers growing into engineers, based on Chapter 8 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers data shapes and formats, database family selection, persistence abstractions, pooling and transactions, consistency models and CAP, caching strategies, scaling, query and index tuning, EXPLAIN, observability, migrations and schema versioning. Use when choosing a format or database, designing persistence, diagnosing slow queries, adding a cache, or planning a migration or schema change. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch08-working-with-data))
- **fse-ch09-software-architecture**: Architecture guidance for engineers growing into design decisions, based on Chapter 9 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Covers trade-off analysis, architecture vs design (strategic vs tactical), quality attributes, stakeholder alignment, monolithic vs distributed styles, evolutionary architecture, hypothesis-driven development, fitness functions, diagrams, ADRs and katas. Use when someone asks which architecture, style or datastore to pick, compares options, writes or lints an ADR, defines fitness functions, or practices architecture. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch09-software-architecture))
- **fse-ch10-to-production**: Production readiness and delivery guidance based on Chapter 10 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers the dev-to-prod gap, performance levers, per-environment config, feature flags, secrets, error handling and logging, HTTPS, authentication, PII, encryption, compliance, Git Flow and other branching models, deployment scripts, rollback, big bang vs gradual rollout, CI/CD, monitoring and maintenance. Use when preparing code for release, reviewing for production readiness, debugging "works on my machine", or designing a deployment pipeline. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch10-to-production))
- **fse-ch11-powering-up-productivity**: Guidance for raising a developer's day-to-day productivity: owning and customizing the IDE, OS and physical setup, command-line and keyboard fluency, timeboxed strategic automation with a payback check, steady habit building, learning from teammates, and personal knowledge management. Based on Chapter 11 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Use when a developer asks how to work faster, set up or tune their environment, learn shortcuts or the shell, decide whether to script a repetitive task, or organize notes and ongoing learning. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch11-powering-up-productivity))
- **fse-ch12-learning-to-learn**: Guides how a developer learns continuously and judges new technology, based on Chapter 12 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering" (O'Reilly, 2025). Covers encoding vs cramming, spaced repetition, diffuse thinking, Shu Ha Ri and Dreyfus stages, learning depth levels, cadence and calendar habits, AI as a study aid, FOMO and hype filtering, tech radars, briefings, workshops and trial projects. Use when building a learning plan, coaching someone stuck or overwhelmed, deciding whether to adopt a hyped tool, or introducing a technology into a team. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch12-learning-to-learn))
- **fse-ch13-soft-skills**: Operational guidance on the durable non-technical skills of software engineering: picking a communication channel, messaging etiquette, running and attending meetings, presenting, writing code as communication, untangling garbled organizational messages, translating tech into business value, influence tactics, power/interest stakeholder mapping, and protecting maker time. Based on Chapter 13 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Use when a developer asks how to communicate, run a meeting, present, persuade, handle stakeholders, or manage focus and time. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch13-soft-skills))
- **fse-ch14-career-management**: Career management guidance for software developers, based on Chapter 14 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Covers discovering interests, IC vs leadership vs hybrid tracks, backward milestone planning, deliberate T-shaped learning with a personal tech radar, life-phase job trade-offs, wins logs, imposter syndrome, networking, interviewing, rejection, burnout and remote work. Use when a developer asks what to learn next, which path or offer to choose, how to prepare for an interview, or how to handle self-doubt, networking or work-life balance. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch14-career-management))
- **fse-ch15-ai-powered-software-engineer**: Guidance for using AI as a developer's pair programmer without giving up engineering judgment, based on Chapter 15 of Schutta and Vega's "Fundamentals of Software Engineering". Covers AI/ML/GenAI/LLM basics, what AI does well and badly, choosing between chatbots, inline IDE assistants and agentic IDEs, prompt engineering, reviewing AI-generated code, avoiding vibe coding, and career positioning. Use when a developer asks how to use AI coding tools, writes or improves a prompt, reviews AI-written code, sets team AI usage rules, or worries AI will replace them. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/fundamentals-of-software-engineering-schutta-vega/fse-ch15-ai-powered-software-engineer))

- **fsk-build**: Constrói a demanda executando o plano de ataque — fatias verticais, cirúrgico, provando cada fatia, commits por intenção e registro para a revisão. - **fsk-debug**: Diagnostica e corrige bug pela causa raiz — loop vermelho primeiro, hipóteses falsificáveis, correção mínima no ponto compartilhado. Dispara em "quebrou", "erro", "não funciona", stack trace, ou algo lento/falhando. Não use para feature nova (fsk-plan/fsk-build) nem para só validar o que já funciona (fsk-prove). ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-debug))
- **fsk-docs**: Gera documentação, apresentações e o tour guiado da mudança no padrão do projeto — relatório de evidências, dossiê de revisão, feature, API, runbook, notas de release, apresentação, relatório executivo. Markdown é escrito direto; formatos binários via docgen MCP quando o projeto o tem, senão pelas skills oficiais de documento (docgen é opcional). Dispara em "documenta", "gera doc/apresentação/relatório", "me guia pela mudança", "tour". Não use para provar (fsk-prove) nem para abrir PR (fsk-ship). Idioma dos artefatos segue o do repo. ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-docs))
- **fsk-explore**: Levanta contexto de uma área e devolve ponteiros (arquivo:linha), nunca conteúdo bruto. Dispara quando o usuário pergunta "onde fica X", "como funciona Y", "o que isso afeta", ou numa área desconhecida ou de impacto amplo antes de planejar/implementar — mas só com pergunta que o Mapa/AGENTS.md do setup NÃO responde (o que o Mapa aponta não se explora de novo). Não use para planejar, implementar, revisar ou provar — só para descobrir e apontar. ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-explore))
- **fsk-learn**: Transforma aprendizado durável em regra viva — convenção do time vira rule/how-to no projeto (por PR, com evidência e carimbo), preferência pessoal vira nota global do usuário, e o Mapa nunca fica falso. Dispara PROATIVAMENTE, no mesmo turno, sempre que algo durável aparece — resposta de grill/entrevista que vale além da demanda, procedimento executado que vai se repetir (vira how-to), correção do usuário, "sempre faça X aqui", padrão repetido, decisão com porquê, drift entre rule e código, e no fecho de TODA fase (plan/build/prove/review/ship/debug/setup) — varre sempre, persiste só o duráve… ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-learn))
- **fsk-plan**: Classifica a demanda por tipo × tamanho × risco, entrevista até o entendimento compartilhado (Combinado) e entrega o plano de ataque que o build executa. Dispara em demanda nova acima de trivial ("quero", "precisamos", "adicionar", "mudar", "criar"). Não use para implementar (é /fsk-build), para bug já localizado (fsk-debug) nem para dúvida de código (fsk-explore). ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-plan))
- **fsk-prove**: Prova comportamento de verdade e sem viés — navegador &gt; API real &gt; integração &gt; unitário (só com motivo escrito); checagem (tsc/lint/unitário) nunca é prova. Lê os critérios antes do diff; em trabalho de risco alto roda em subagente que não viu a implementação. Dispara em "valida", "testa", "funciona?", ao terminar/fechar uma mudança de código e no fechamento de cada fatia. Não use para revisar código (fsk-review) nem para migrar a suíte existente. ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-prove))
- **fsk-review**: Verifica a mudança contra o plano e as diretrizes — fidelidade (nada faltou, nada fora), regras, segurança, impacto, simplicidade; bloqueia regra de negócio alterada sem aprovação e reverte/ajusta ruído, overengineering e antipadrão no diff. Dispara em "revisa", "está bom?", "review". Não use para provar comportamento (fsk-prove) nem para abrir PR (fsk-ship). ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-review))
- **fsk-setup**: Prepara o repositório para o workflow fsk — detecta o modo (novo/brownfield/auditoria), explora com evidência, entrevista o que o repo não responde (inclusive o fluxo de git do time), gera AGENTS.md + rules + estrutura .claude e propõe ferramentas/scripts com "ok". Dispara quando o repo não tem AGENTS.md ou o usuário pede para configurar/preparar o projeto. Não use para planejar nem implementar demanda — isso é /fsk-plan e /fsk-build. ([KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit](https://github.com/KauaLealz/fullstack-skills-kit/tree/HEAD/skills/fsk-setup))
- **fsk-ship**: Entrega a mudança seguindo o fluxo de git do time à risca — dossiê de revisão, rodada final "está 100%?", push/PR no formato do repo com evidência e Mapa atualizado no mesmo PR. Dispara por "abre PR", "entrega", "manda pro main", "push" (o agente sugere, nunca aciona sozinho; o commit local é do /fsk-build).
